Optimizing wireless sensor networks using fuzzy triangular snake graph models and fuzzy topological indices
Authors: Atef F. Hashem, Shama Liaqat, Zeeshan Saleem Mufti, Muhammad Farhan Hanif
Published: 2025

ABSTRACT
This research focuses on a detailed investigation of advanced fuzzy indices applied to enhanced version of triangular snake graph, designated as fuzzy triangular snake graph (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}). Its practical applications in wireless sensor network (WSN) is also designed. We derive closed-form formulas for several fuzzy indices, including first and second fuzzy Zagreb indices, fuzzy Harmonic, Randić, Misbalance Prodeg, Nirmala, along with Sombor indices. By applying these indices to a WSN modeled with redundant triangular connectivity, we evaluate the network’s structural efficiency, robustness, and fault tolerance. The computed results reveal that the fuzzy triangular snake topology ensures balanced node connectivity, minimizes energy imbalance, and enhances the network’s ability to maintain reliable communication in the presence of node failures and uncertainties. The results confirm the applicability of the fuzzy triangular snake model in optimization of energy- and resilient WSNs, thus drawing a high degree of correlation between such contemporary graph theory, as fuzziness, and the design of a modern communication network.

Introduction

In the theory of complex systems, graph theory is an indispensable part that provides a recoding of structural qualities of graph in terms of numerical quantities that model relationships and interactions in many fields of activity, such as computer science, chemical science, biology, and social sciences. Such indices have been broadly applied to network analysis, and in chemoinformatics in the estimation of molecular properties. The conventional topological indices are generally characterized in crisp graphs, in which edges are either present or absent with no potential of confusion. Nevertheless, uncertainty, imprecision, and vagueness are the features of many real systems, and they cannot be properly represented by using crisp graph structures. Fuzzy graph theory addresses this shortcoming by generalizing the classical notions of a graph by allowing not only the edges but also the nodes to be vague, the degrees to which they are vague capturing the uncertainty inherent in the system. Fuzzy topological descriptors are extensions of the crisp topological indices in this direction, giving a previously degree of detail in the analysis of the structures of graphs into which the edges have changing value of strength as opposed to being strictly two state system. Such descriptors are especially handy in situations where clarity in the relationship between components is lacking or there is a reason to describe the uncertainty of the strength of interactions. This paper was dedicated to the evaluation of the fuzzy topological indices of the particular graph called triangular snake graph. Serving as key tool in social netwrok analysis, the graph reflects the organization of central entity interacting with multiple clusters. In our study, we intend to analyze the fuzzy topological estimates applied to graph and how the estimates reflect the structural distinctions brought in the graph by the notion of fuzziness. Adopting a systematic approach to compare descriptors within fuzzy and classical framework, we expect to learn how fuzziness can contribute to the analysis of graphs. In addition, we discuss possible areas of use of such indices, like social network analysis where the uncertainty of connection is one of the most important aspects. The results of the work are added to a developing body of research and utilization s in fuzzy graph theory, and it offers insight into the structural analysis of complex network that possesses a considerable level of the uncertainty. The novality of this work lies in the first comprehensive investigation of fuzzy triangular snake graph through wide range of fuzzy topological indices, which has not been previously reported in the literature. Unlike prior studies that focused on individual indices or simpler graph structures, this research develops generalized closed form formulas for multiple indices and validates their utility through practical case study in wireless sensor networks.

The motivation for this study arises from the need to address uncertainty in modern communication and sensor networks. Traditional graphs fails to capture the imprecision present in real world systems, particularly in wireless sensor networks. Where node failures variable link strengths, and energy imbalance are common challenges. The fuzzy triangular snake graph offers a promising structure that integrates redundancy and balanced connectivity. By applying fuzzy topological indices to this structure, we aim to evaluate how fuzziness can enhance the modeling of robustness, fault tolerance, and energy efficiency in wireless networks. thus providing both theoretical significance and practical applicability.

Literature review

Leonhard Euler is generally credited with the beginnings of the graph theory, having in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1736$$\end{document} solved the well-known Konigsberg Bridges Problem. In work, at \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1736$$\end{document} is considered first paper in graph theory1.

During the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$19^{th}$$\end{document}century, more grew, notably the theory of electrical networks, and tree enumeration2. The theorem of Kuratowski offers a characterization of planar graph, stamping both the theoretical and applied reader of the graph theory3. Development of key algorithms for graph traversal, shortest path, and maximum flow has expanded the practical utilization s of graph theory4–6.

Parallelly, Lotfi Zadeh’s overview of fuzzy set based theory in 1965 marked a paradigm shift in dealing with uncertainty, allowing for graduation in set membership7–9.

Zadeh10 introduced a fuzzy set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {A}$$\end{document} of a universal set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}$$\end{document}. Zadeh’s fuzzy sets generalize this ides by defining a membership mapping \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _\mathcal {A}(x)$$\end{document} which gives the membership grade of each element \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x$$\end{document} in a fuzzy set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {A}$$\end{document} as a value between \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1$$\end{document}. This allows for the representation of sets that have partial membership, reflecting the uncertainty or vagueness of real world notions11. In fuzzy set, membership mapping plays a key role by specifying how each member in the universal set is assigned a particular degree of association.

Building on fuzzy sets, fuzzy relations were introduced as an approach to extend the notion of connection among elements in fuzzy sets. Fuzzy relations generalize classical relations by allowing partial membership. A fuzzy relation is written as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {R}$$\end{document} on a pair of sets \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {Y}$$\end{document}, characterizing the degree of association between their elements. membership mapping \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _{\mathcal {R}}:\mathcal {X}\times \mathcal {Y}\rightarrow [0,1]$$\end{document}, showing the strength of relationship among each pair \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y)$$\end{document} in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}\times \mathcal {Y}$$\end{document}.

Singh et al. identified numerous and varied real-life examples of fuzzy logic being applied in the defense, healthcare, as well as control systems sectors, and proved that it was very powerful in approaching uncertainty and complexity in situations involving decision making12.

The fuzzy graph theory extends the traditional graph theory with ideas of flexibility of fuzzy sets. The fuzzy graphs structure was initially created by Rosendfeld in 1975. Classical graph theory models relationships using edges between nodes, with edges either existing or not. In contrast, a fuzzy graph allows for edges and nodes to exist to varying degrees, specified by membership mappings. Fuzzy graphs are specified by a collection of nodes along with fuzzy relation that shows the edges. Following Rosenfeld’s pioneering work13, fuzzy graph theory witnessed rapid development through the contributions of fuzzy versions of classical graph theory principles, including fuzzy paths, fuzzy trees, and fuzzy connectedness14,15.

Topological indices involve numerical representations, it derives its basis on molecular arrangements of a chemical substance that indicates its topology and are used in the study of chemical properties and molecular structures. The research conducted by Harold Wiener, published in 1947, is a foundational work within the domain of chemical graph theory. This is what Wiener also presented in this study whereby what is now called the Wiener index, is a famous topological descriptor which is applied to generating the boiling points of the paraffin16. In the 1972 a paper published by Gutman and Trinajstić, in which authors applied graph theory to evaluate the total \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pi$$\end{document}-electron energy of alternate hydrocarbons17.They used the adjacency matrix of molecule’s graph to compute its molecular orbital and related energy levels. In 1975 Milan Randic proposed the descriptor that is currently called the Randic descriptor or Randic connectivity descriptor, a topological descriptor used to quantify the branching of molecular structures18. Randic index is expressed as summation of reciprocals of square roots of the degrees(number of bonds) of neighboring atoms in the molecular graph. Such a descriptor captures the extent of branching in a molecule, particularly in hydrocarbons, and has been shown to correlate with various chemical and physical properties, such as molecular stability and reactivity. In19,20, researches contributed to the broader utilization s of graph theory in chemical science and molecular modeling. Mondal and Pal explored the structural properties of graphene through the lens of graph theory21. They introduced and applied novel degree-based descriptors to characterize the molecular structures of graphene. In another research, they focused on defining and computing novel indices, which provide deeper insights into the structural properties of molecules22. Amin and Nayeem studied F-index and F-coindex of line graphs of subdivision graphs23. Idrees et al. explored how topological descriptors can be used as effective tool in drug discovery, particularly for eye-related disease24. In25, Hasani, M., and Ghods, M. investigated the relationship between topological descriptors and QSPR for chemical compound used in treating heart disease.

Traditional graph theory has well-established indices that characterize the structure and properties of graphs, However, as the use of fuzzy logic has grown in various fields, there was a need to extend these concepts to fuzzy graphs, which represent uncertainty and vagueness in relationships. In the 2020 research, Kalathian investigated various topological descriptors specifically for fuzzy graphs26. The authors focused on extending classical topological descriptors, such as the degree-based connectivity indices, to the fuzzy context.They introduced new indices that account for the values of membership of nodes and edges, enhancing the understanding of the underlying characteristics of fuzzy graph structures. In 2021, Atef and Nawar analyzed the interplay between fuzzy graphs and their corresponding topological models. The authors investigated how fuzzy graphs can be used to define and study fuzzy topological descriptors, enhancing the understanding of both concepts27.

Islam and Pal in another research introduced the Hyper-Wiener descriptor specifically for fuzzy graphs along with explored its practical utilization s, particulary in the context of the share market28. In the 2022, Mufti et al. focused on calculating the first along with second fuzzy Zagreb indices for linear and multi cyclic hydrocarbons29. The authors presented results of their calculations for various hydrocarbons structures, illustrating how the fuzzy Zagreb indices can provide valuable insights into the molecular topology. In30, the authors used fuzzy Randic and fuzzy Harmonic descriptores to solve the issue of cyber crime and how cyber crime indices can be used to command patterns and connection and also weakness in the cyber network.

Subsequently, Tabraiz et al. calculated and studied a number of fuzzy topological descriptors, particular to fuzzy flower graph. These indices quantifies several structural features of fuzzy flower graph giving us a clue about connectivity and character31. Islam and Pal also gave a complex mathematical expression to compute the F-index in fuzzy graphs (in which they explained the influence of membership levels of nodes and edges on the computation of the descriptor)32. Mufti et al., have defined and calculated some of the fuzzy topological descriptors of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$qC_n$$\end{document} graph in 2023, which give insights into its structural properties such as connectivity and branching33. In34 authors developed a approach for multi criteria decision making which utilizes the defined fuzzy indices, This framework helps in assessing and priortizing alternatives based on multiple criteria, reflecting the uncertainty inherent in the data.

The paper presented by Mufti provided a thorough mathematical analysis of the fuzzy indices, exploring their properties and relationships within the context of the pizza graph35. In, researchers provided a detailed mathematical formulation for calculating the second Zagreb descriptor in fuzzy graphs, along with relevant properties and theorems36. In37, authors proposed a methodological framework that integrate fuzzy topological descriptors such as connectivity descriptor, average connectivity descriptor and randic index for fuzzy graph. In38, researchers seeks to reveal insight into the topological properties of fuzzy graphs by examining numerious topological descriptors.

A study by Jamil39,40, introduces an innovative utilization s of intuitionistic fuzzy graph theory to enhance the efficiency of vaccination centers through fuzzy topological descriptors. In41, the Gutman descriptor, based on degree and distance is studied for fuzzy graphs. Relationship between Gutman index of a fuzzy graph, its subgraph, and complete fuzzy graph are explored.Bounds are also established for different classes of fuzzy graphs. Uzma et al. introduces Wiener index-based energies for fuzzy graphs using fuzzy wiener matrices and their eigenvalues. Bounds are established, and after that the approach is applied to a multi-criteria decision making problem42. Mirza et al. introduced polynomial regression, a machine learning technique, to create polynomials for adjacency and degree matrices in the fuzzy conjugate graph of dihedral group, and refineing the calculations43. Recently, Liaqat et al. introduces the fuzzy Misbalance Prodeg index, a abstracted variant of the traditional Misbalance Prodeg index used in crisp graph theory44. This research establishes bounds for the given fuzzy descriptor and analyze specific graph classes. Authors proposes new topological descriptors, including two versions of the Sombor index, specifically designed for the Intuitionistic fuzzy graph framework to enhance their practical utilization s45.

Recently, fuzzy graph based models have been applied to facility location problem, where fuzzy node cover concept combined with particle swarm optimization were utilized to handle real life situations such as emergency air craft landing efficiency46.

Fuzzy graphoidal covers have been introduced as novel graph theoretic parameter, integrated with neural networks to enhance cancer cancer type prediction in medical decision making47.

A novel concept of pseudo vertex covering sets in fuzzy structures has been applied to evaluate India’s performance towards sustainable development goal Gender Eqaulity, providing a new insights into socio-economics network analysis48.

Sun et al.40 proposed the live migration strategies for multiple correlated virtual machines to minimize the migration time, downtime, and the traffic overhead in cloud data centers. Yang et al.49 presented uLiDR (ubiquitous intelligent indoor location discovery), which is an energy-efficient indoor pedestrian navigation system using both inertial sensors and unmodulated visible light in mobile and smartphone devices. Wang et al.50 proposed a fast and lightweight similarity detection method which could enhance the post-deduplication delta compression throughput by efficiently detecting similar data chunks among parts of the data streams. Wang et al.51 proposed a low-light image dehazing physical model based recovery method of natural color image and visibility recovery. Xu et al.52 considered the energy optimization problem in wirelessly powered IoT sensors and proposed to combine deep reinforcement learning with the session-specific energy-efficient data collection. Gan et al.53 proposed TianheGraph, a graph search system which is able to scale the BFS performance and efficiency on Tianhe supercomputer system. In fact, in view of the graph processing efficiency, Gan et al.54also introduced a degree-equalized CSR format GraphCSR to balance the workload and accelerate large-scale computations. IXP Networks and Services (Xu et al.55) Meta - Memory-efficient polynomial multiplication accelerator using two-dimensional coupled-BFUs to achieve high throughput with reduced memory Li et al.56 proposed distribution regression for wireless telecommunication reliability forecasting, which has higher accuracy and can deal with uncertain information. Finally, Xiao et al.57 pushed further on the task-oriented V2X information sharing to solve occlusion issues for autonomous vehicles, to greatly improve the perception, safety, and situation awareness.

In this article, we present fuzzy analogues of usual topological descriptors to fuzzy triangular snake graph. We formulate and investigate fuzzy analogues of familiar indices, with a specific focus to the fuzzy interpretation of the properties of those types of graphs. This paper seeks to make contributions to the more general knowing fuzzy graph theory and its usage with the formulation and analysis of the designed fuzzy topological descriptors. The findings herein provide a source of extension of knowledge to structural analysis of a fuzzy graph, and thus present new areas of research and practical utilization in a variety of domains.Fig. 1Triangular snake graph for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}=2$$\end{document}.Fig. 2Triangular snake graph for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}=3$$\end{document}.Fig. 3Triangular snake graph for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}=3$$\end{document}.

Triangular snake graph for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}=2$$\end{document}.

Preliminaries

A graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {G} = (\mathbb {V}, \mathbb {E})$$\end{document} is precisely expressed as two sets ordered, whereby \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {V}$$\end{document} represents the non empty collection of nodes and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {E}$$\end{document} is the sets of edges which is expressed as an uncoupled couple of different nodes. The basic units of the graph are called nodes and the connection between these units is demonstrated by the edges.A Triangular snake graph is special type of path graph, connecting triangular subgraph in a linear sequence. Triangular snake graph of order \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}$$\end{document} has, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}$$\end{document} triangles arranged in chain, in which each triangle shares exactly one node with next triangle. We reach a fuzzy Sombor index by considering both the fuzziness of the nodes, as well as of links, to represent a fuzzified graph version of the classical Sombor index. For the fuzzy graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\breve{F} = \bigl (\breve{V}, \breve{E} \bigr )$$\end{document}, the corresponding fuzzy Sombor index is defined by: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal{S}\mathcal{O}(\breve{F} )= & \sum _{(\breve{b},\breve{a}) \in \breve{E}} \sqrt{ \left( d(\breve{b}) \cdot \tau (\breve{b})\right) ^2 + \left( d(\breve{a}) \cdot \tau (\breve{a})\right) ^2 } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

We reach a fuzzy Sombor index by considering both the fuzziness of the nodes, as well as of links, to represent a fuzzified graph version of the classical Sombor index. For the fuzzy graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\breve{F} = \bigl (\breve{V}, \breve{E} \bigr )$$\end{document}, the corresponding fuzzy Sombor index is defined by: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal{S}\mathcal{O}(\breve{F} )= & \sum _{(\breve{b},\breve{a}) \in \breve{E}} \sqrt{ \left( d(\breve{b}) \cdot \tau (\breve{b})\right) ^2 + \left( d(\breve{a}) \cdot \tau (\breve{a})\right) ^2 } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

As a topological indicator, the fuzzy Nirmala index helps in examining the structural configuration of fuzzy graph. Based upon a fuzzy graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\breve{F} = (\breve{V}, \breve{E})$$\end{document} we employ the fuzzy Nirmala index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}(\breve{F} )$$\end{document} as: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {N}(\breve{F} )= & \sum _{(\breve{b},\breve{a}) \in \breve{E}} \sqrt{\tau (\breve{b})\cdot d(\breve{b})+\tau (\breve{a})\cdot d(\breve{a})} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

As a topological indicator, the fuzzy Nirmala index helps in examining the structural configuration of fuzzy graph. Based upon a fuzzy graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\breve{F} = (\breve{V}, \breve{E})$$\end{document} we employ the fuzzy Nirmala index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}(\breve{F} )$$\end{document} as: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {N}(\breve{F} )= & \sum _{(\breve{b},\breve{a}) \in \breve{E}} \sqrt{\tau (\breve{b})\cdot d(\breve{b})+\tau (\breve{a})\cdot d(\breve{a})} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Fuzzy triangular snake graph for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}=2$$\end{document}.

Fuzzy triangular snake graph for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}=3$$\end{document}.

Fuzzy triangular snake graph for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}=4$$\end{document}.

The weighting strategy is an implementation of the fact that the nodes, which are more influential or connected structurally, have greater dominance to the fuzzy topology. Also due to the inversely proportional nature of the weights involving the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}$$\end{document} number, as the graph gets larger the influence of the nodes is kept relative to scale and therefore as the order of the graph increases the normalization of the influence is kept the same.

The fuzzy triangular snake graph and fuzzy intersection graph differ both in structure and in the behavior of their topological indices. The fuzzy trianular snake graph is built by associating \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}$$\end{document} triangles along path, which introduces redundancy and cyclic connectivity, making it particularly suitable for modeling fault tolerant networks. In contrast fuzzy intersection graphs are constructed by considering intersections among sets, and thus their connectivity is evaluated by the degree of overlap between them. When comparing fuzzy indices fuzzy triangular snake graph usually yields higher values of indices due to the presence of triangular redundancy, which increase both degree based and distance based measures. While fuzzy intersection graphs tend to reflect variability depending on the degree of intersection, so their indices capture heterogeneity rather than redundancy.

Theorem 1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} be the fuzzy triangular snake graph and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}\ge 1$$\end{document}. after that, in the case of fuzzy triangular snake graph, the first fuzzy Zagreb index of the fuzzy triangular snake graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {FTS}}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} is expressed as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {M}_1(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & \frac{1}{8\mathbb {N}^3}\cdot \left[ 3\mathbb {N}+5\right] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Theorem 2

Given a fuzzy triangular snake graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N} \ge 1$$\end{document}, the corresponding second fuzzy Zagreb index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_2(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})$$\end{document} is expressed by:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {M}_2(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}) = \frac{8\mathbb {N}+5}{64\mathbb {N}^4}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

\usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\breve{a}$$\end{document}, in that order. In the case of fuzzy triangular snake graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {M}_2(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & 2\left[ \frac{1}{2}\left[ \left( \frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{3}{4\mathbb {N}}\right) \left( \frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{3}{4\mathbb {N}}\right) \right] \right] +4\left[ \frac{1}{2}\left[ \left( \frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{3}{4\mathbb {N}}\right) \left( \frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}\right) \right] \right] + \\ & \left( 2\mathbb {N}-4\right) \left[ \frac{1}{2}\left[ \left( \frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}\right) \left( \frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}\right) \right] \right] +(\mathbb {N}-2)\left[ \frac{1}{2}\left[ \left( \frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}\right) \left( \frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}\right) \right] \right] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}refineing the above expression, we get:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {M}_2(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & \frac{9}{64\mathbb {N}^4}+\frac{3}{16\mathbb {N}^4}+\frac{(\mathbb {N}-2)}{16\mathbb {N}^4}+\frac{(\mathbb {N}-2)}{16\mathbb {N}^4} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}taking common similar terms:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} This results shows that the lower values of the second Zagreb index correspond to reduced structural complexity, that helps simplify routing and connectivity management in large networks. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Theorem 3

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} act as fuzzy triangular snake graph possessing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N} \ge 1$$\end{document}. Thus fuzzy Harmonic index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})$$\end{document} for the fuzzy triangular snake graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} is formulated as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {H}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & \frac{\mathbb {N}^2}{15}\left[ 45\mathbb {N}-22\right] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {H}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & \frac{\mathbb {N}^2}{15}\left[ 45\mathbb {N}-22\right] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}This ends the proof of the theorem. The high values of fuzzy harmonić index demonstrate redundancy in connectivity, ensuring that communication remains stable even when certain links fail. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Theorem 4

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} serve as a fuzzy triangular snake graph possessing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N} \ge 1$$\end{document}. The fuzzy Randić index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {R}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})$$\end{document} with respect to the fuzzy triangular snake graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} is formulated as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {R}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & 2\mathbb {N}^2\left[ \frac{4}{3}+\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}+3\mathbb {N}-6\right] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Proof

Assume that the fuzzy triangular snake graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N} \ge 1$$\end{document}, which is be composed of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2\mathbb {N} + 1$$\end{document} nodes along with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$3\mathbb {N}$$\end{document} links. The graph could be interpreted according to and on the bases of the form of edges and their relevant degrees and matching set of values of the nodes. The cumulative \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$3\mathbb {N}$$\end{document} links in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2$$\end{document} edges connect nodes with weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document},and degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{3}{4\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$4$$\end{document} edges connect nodes both having weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}},\frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} and degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{3}{4\mathbb {N}},\frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2\mathbb {N}-4$$\end{document} edges connect nodes endowed with weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}},\frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} and degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}},\frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}-2$$\end{document} edges connect nodes both having weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} and degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}. For the fuzzy triangular snake graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}, the fuzzy Randić index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {R}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})$$\end{document} is given by:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {R}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & \frac{1}{2} \sum _{(\breve{b},\breve{a}) \in E} \frac{1}{\sqrt{\tau (\breve{b}) \cdot d(\breve{b}) \cdot \tau (\breve{a}) \cdot d(\breve{a}) }} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}it calculates an inverse square root of a product of the membership grades and degrees of the nodes inside an edge of the two nodes denoted by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\breve{b}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\breve{a}$$\end{document}. For any given edge type the fuzzy Randić index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {R}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})$$\end{document} is evaluated as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \left( \frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{3}{4\mathbb {N}}\right) }}\right] +4\cdot \left[ \frac{1}{2}\cdot \frac{1}{\sqrt{\left( \frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{3}{4\mathbb {N}}\right) \cdot \left( \cdot \left( \frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}\right) }}\right] + (\mathbb {N}-2)\cdot \left[ \frac{1}{2}\cdot \frac{1}{\sqrt{\left( \frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}\right) \cdot \left( \frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}\right) }}\right] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}upon simplification, we obtain:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {R}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & \frac{1}{\sqrt{(\frac{3}{8\mathbb {N}^2})^2}}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{\frac{3}{2\cdot 4^2\cdot \mathbb {N}^4}}}+\frac{(\mathbb {N}-2)}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{4^2\cdot \mathbb {N}^4}}}+\frac{(\mathbb {N}-2)}{2\sqrt{(\frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}^2})^2}} \\= & \frac{8\mathbb {N}^2}{3}+\frac{2\cdot 4\sqrt{2}\mathbb {N}^2}{\sqrt{3}}+4\mathbb {N}^2(\mathbb {N}-2)+\frac{4\mathbb {N}^2(\mathbb {N}-2)}{2} \\= & \mathbb {N}^2\left[ \frac{8}{3}+\frac{8\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}+4\mathbb {N}-8+2\mathbb {N}-4\right] \\ \mathcal {R}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & 2\mathbb {N}^2\left[ \frac{4}{3}+\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{3}}+3\mathbb {N}-6\right] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus, the theorem has been proven.

The increasing trend of fuzzy randic index highlights strong connectivity patterns, capturing the robustness of the triangular redundancy in the network. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Theorem 5

Proof

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} This results highlights moderate structural complexity, showing that the network achieves a balance bewteen redundancy and efficiency \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Theorem 8

The fuzzy Sombor index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{O}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})$$\end{document} for a fuzzy triangular snake graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} is expressed as, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N} \ge 1$$\end{document}, is formulated as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal{S}\mathcal{O}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & \frac{3\sqrt{2}\mathbb {N}-3\sqrt{2}+2\sqrt{13}}{4\mathbb {N}^2} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Proof

Assume that the fuzzy triangular snake graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} involving the following properties: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2$$\end{document} edges connect nodes with weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document},and degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{3}{4\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$4$$\end{document} edges connect nodes both having weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}},\frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} and degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{3}{4\mathbb {N}},\frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2\mathbb {N}-4$$\end{document} edges connect nodes having weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}},\frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} and degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}},\frac{1}{n}$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}-2$$\end{document} edges connect nodes both having weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} and degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}. The fuzzy Sombor index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{O}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})$$\end{document} for a fuzzy triangular snake graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document} is expressed as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal{S}\mathcal{O}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & \sum _{(\breve{b},\breve{a}) \in E} \sqrt{ \left( d(\breve{b}) \cdot \tau (\breve{b})\right) ^2 + \left( d(\breve{a}) \cdot \tau (\breve{a})\right) ^2 } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Now for any given edge we evaluate the fuzzy Sombor index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{O}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})$$\end{document} as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal{S}\mathcal{O}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & 2\left[ \sqrt{\left( \frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{3}{4\mathbb {N}}\right) ^2+\left( \frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{3}{4\mathbb {N}}\right) ^2}\right] +4 \left[ \sqrt{\left( \frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{3}{4\mathbb {N}}\right) ^2+\left( \frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}\right) ^2}\right] + \\ & (2\mathbb {N}-4)\left[ \sqrt{\left( \frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{2\mathbb {N}}\right) ^2+\left( \frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}\right) ^2}\right] +(\mathbb {N}-2)\left[ \sqrt{\left( \frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}\right) ^2+\left( \frac{1}{4\mathbb {N}}\cdot \frac{1}{\mathbb {N}}\right) ^2}\right] \\= & 2\left[ \sqrt{2}(\frac{3}{8\mathbb {N}^2})\right] +4\left[ \sqrt{\frac{9}{64\mathbb {N}^4}+\frac{1}{16\mathbb {N}^2}}\right] +2(\mathbb {N}-2)\left[ \sqrt{\frac{1}{16\mathbb {N}^4}+\frac{1}{16\mathbb {N}^4}}\right] +(\mathbb {N}-2)\left[ \sqrt{\frac{1}{16\mathbb {N}^4}+\frac{1}{16\mathbb {N}^4}}\right] \\= & \frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4\mathbb {N}^2}+4\sqrt{\frac{9+4}{64\mathbb {N}^4}}+2(\mathbb {N}-2)\sqrt{\frac{1+1}{16\mathbb {N}^4}}+(\mathbb {N}-2)\sqrt{\frac{1+1}{16\mathbb {N}^4}} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}after further simplification:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal{S}\mathcal{O}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & \frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4\mathbb {N}^2}+\frac{4\sqrt{13}}{8\mathbb {N}^2}+\frac{2(\mathbb {N}-2)\sqrt{2}}{4\mathbb {N}^2}+\frac{(\mathbb {N}-2)\sqrt{2}}{4\mathbb {N}^2} \\= & \frac{3\sqrt{2}}{4\mathbb {N}^2}+\frac{\sqrt{13}}{2\mathbb {N}^2}+\frac{(\mathbb {N}-2)\sqrt{2}}{2\mathbb {N}^2}+\frac{(\mathbb {N}-2)\sqrt{2}}{4\mathbb {N}^2} \\= & \frac{3\sqrt{2}+2\sqrt{13}+2\sqrt{2}(\mathbb {N}-2)+\sqrt{2}(\mathbb {N}-2)}{4\mathbb {N}^2} \\ \mathcal{S}\mathcal{O}(\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}})= & \frac{3\sqrt{2}\mathbb {N}-3\sqrt{2}+2\sqrt{13}}{4\mathbb {N}^2} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus, the theorem has been proven.

The fuzzy sombor index captures combined effects of node degrees and fuzziness, reflecting both robustness and structural symmetry of the triangular snake graph. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

Applications in communication network modeling

Wireless Sensor Networks (WSNs) are extensively utilized in diverse utilization s, including environmental observation, pipeline monitoring, along with infrastructure condition assessment. In this case, we model a WSN deployed along a pipeline with redundant triangular connectivity at every 5 intervals, which forms a fuzzy triangular snake graph. This configuration ensures that each sensor node is connected to its neighboring nodes through triangular relationships, providing a redundant communication structure. Such a topology improves the networks’s robustness and fault tolerance, especially in environments with potential node failure or signal degradation.

Overview of the communication model

In a WSNs each node represents a sensor that collects data (e.g., temperature, pressure, or humidity), and transmits this data to neighboring nodes. The overlapping connectivity in a triangle also makes sure that in the event of certain communication link failure, such data would still go through via other paths hence enhancing reliability and robustness. The fuzzy (triangular snake graph) is applicable in this case in the modeling of uncertain and dynamic characteristics of sensor node connections and communication paths where the edges of the triangular snake graph have a certain degree of uncertainty (fuzziness).

Fuzzy indices in network optimization

Fuzzy topological indices computed on the fuzzy triangular snake graph are useful in the determination of efficiency, resiliency and fault tolerance of the communication network. These indices are of importance in gaining insight about the structural properties of the network and this way the designing and the performance of the network can be and is optimized in uncertain environments. The Fig. 7 is a wireless sensor network (WSN) of the line with a triangular redundant connection at each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$5^{th}$$\end{document} step deployed on a pipeline. The sensor nodes are triangularly connected to the neighboring nodes thereby creating a triangular snake graph in which there is a degree of fuzziness. When n = 5, after that there are 5 different triangles in the arrangement that give it a redundant path of communication to be robust.Fig. 7Model of a wireless sensor network (WSN) deployed over a pipeline into a fuzzy triangular snake garph possessing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N} = 5$$\end{document} triangles.

Model of a wireless sensor network (WSN) deployed over a pipeline into a fuzzy triangular snake garph possessing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N} = 5$$\end{document} triangles.

To apply the specified formula (first fuzzy Zagreb index) and compute for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N} = 5$$\end{document} we get:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {M}_1(\mathcal {FTS}_{5})= & \frac{1}{8\mathbb {N}^3}\cdot \left( 3\mathbb {N}+5\right) = \frac{1}{8 \cdot 5^3} \cdot (3 \cdot 5 + 5) = 0.0200. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}This is a low descriptor value defining a balanced and uniformly distributed degree within fuzzy WSN topology. When \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_1$$\end{document} is lower, the energy imbalance is minimized within sensor nodes and is essential in extending network life-time and in maintaining performance consistency. Thus, the descriptor calculated proves the effectiveness of applying a fuzzy triangular snake model in designing an energy-saving communication network.

Using the given formula describing the second fuzzy Zagreb index we have:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {M}_2(\mathcal {FTS}_{5})= & \frac{8 \cdot 5 + 5}{64 \cdot 5^4} = \frac{40 + 5}{64 \cdot 625} = \frac{45}{40000} = 0.0011. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}As low a value of descriptor would mean that there is a low product of the degree over edges of fuzzy WSN topology. This is one of the characteristics that would require less complexity in routing and maintaining links between nodes. Consequently, the calculated descriptor can justify strong robustness and scalability of the fuzzy triangular snake model, and hence, it would be applicable in large-scale communication infrastructure whose connectivity nature remains quite important and crucial in maintaining efficiency.

With \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N} = 5$$\end{document} applying a given formula of the fuzzy Harmonic index we obtain:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {H}(\mathcal {FTS}_{5})= & \frac{5^2}{15} \cdot \left( 45 \cdot 5 - 22\right) = \frac{25}{15} \cdot (225 - 22) = \frac{25}{15} \cdot 203 = \frac{25 \cdot 203}{15} = \frac{5075}{15} = 338.3333. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Such a greater value of the descriptor shows a high degree of two-way connectivity in the fuzzy WSN topology and this leaves a provision to ensure that even the nodes that are far apart can experience an effective communication channel. The higher Harmonic index indicates better reliability, redundancy, which implies that fuzzy triangular snake model is beneficial in the design of fault-tolerant communications networks.

Using the formula that has been defined, we can evaluate the fuzzy Misbalance Prodeg index at \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N}=5$$\end{document} as:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal{M}\mathcal{P}(\mathcal {FTS}_{5})= & \frac{3 \cdot 5 - 4 + 2\sqrt{6}}{5} = \frac{15 - 4 + 2 \cdot 2.4495}{5} = \frac{11 + 4.899}{5} = \frac{15.899}{5} = 3.1798. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}This value implies that the disparity in the degree of nodes is rather small meaning that the network is balanced in regard of connectivity of the nodes, which adds to the energy efficiency and load balance.

Applying the defined formula for the fuzzy Nirmala index, we compute for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {N} = 5$$\end{document}:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {N}(\mathcal {FTS}_{5})= & \frac{3 \cdot 5 - 6 + \sqrt{6} + 2\sqrt{5}}{\sqrt{2} \cdot 5} = \frac{15 - 6 + 2.4495 + 2 \cdot 2.2361}{7.0711} = \frac{9 + 2.4495 + 4.4722}{7.0711} = \frac{15.9217}{7.0711} = 2.2514. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}This descriptor value indicates a moderate structural complexity of the fuzzy wireless sensor network that is modelled on the triangular snake graph. When a fuzzy Nirmala index is between these limits after that there is an optimum to balance between the connectivity richness and the structural efficiency. It assures that the network has adequate redundancy in interconnections among nodes such that it is both failure ready without being unduly redundant and costly to deploy or use. Therefore, the calculated descriptor confirms the feasibility of efficient and robust communication networks involving the use of fuzzy triangular snake models further. Using these fuzzy topological indices we acn draw informative conclusions concerning the structure and performance of fuzzy triangular snake graph in WSN environment.

Conclusion

This research is focused towards the description and evaluation of fuzzy topological descriptors in order to Fuzzy triangular snake graph (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {FTS}_{\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}) such as first fuzzy Zagreb index, second fuzzy Zagreb index, fuzzy Harmonic index, fuzzy Randic index, fuzzy Misbalance Prodeg index, fuzzy Nirmala index and fuzzy Sombor index. The derived generalized formulae of each descriptor present the mathematical background of the internal properties of the fuzzy triangular snake graph. To show the practical utilization s of these indices we simulated a wireless sensor network (WSN) that was deployed along a pipeline using the fuzzy triangular snake graph structure. We assessed the balance, robustness, fault tolerance and energy efficient performance of the networks by computing the indices that were calculated. The findings affirms that fuzzy triangular snake model of redundant triangular connectivity guarantees reduced energy imbalance, higher reliability and fault tolerance- as a result of the unpredictable and adaptive nature of real world WSN utilization. The literature contributes to a fresh connection between fuzzy graph theory and modeling communication networks. These observed indices not only shed more insights into the structure but they are also used to optimize design of energy aware and resilient WSNs. The potential future work could build on this framework, evaluating the utilization s of these indices to other network architecture, discussing the possibility of dynamic reconfiguration strategies and confirming the models by means of simulation and real-life implementation.

Future directions

This framework can be extended to the domain of m-polar fuzzy graphs, where each vertex and edge is associated with multiple membership values instead of single one. In such cases the definitions of fuzzy topological indices would be modified by incorporating the aggregated contributions of m-polar memberships. This extension would provide a richer and more flexible representation of uncertainty, offering more potential for analyzing complex real world systems under multiple levels of vagueness.

Another promising extension of this study lies in the development of energy based descriptors for the fuzzy triangular snake graph. Graph energy usually derived from the eigen values of adjacency or Lapcian matrices, can be reformulated in the fuzzy setting by considering membership weighted matrices. By correlating these spectral measures with the fuzzy indices computed in this work, one could design new hybrid decriptor that highlight both algebraic and combinatorial properties. Such an approach would enrich the analysis of fuzzy triangular snake graph and broaden their applications in energy efficient network.
